Lately, it's been the complete opposite—I just have this overwhelming energy and keep jumping from one big, impulsive plan to the next.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a similar period of unusually high energy, racing thoughts, or decreased need for sleep at any other time in your life before this month?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall ever having an episode quite like this before in my life. This level of intense energy and constant planning has really only surfaced over the past month, ever since I started facing that retirement transition.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen a doctor or therapist for your mood in the past, or been prescribed any medications to help with how you were feeling?\n\nPatient: No, I've never seen a doctor or a therapist for my mood in the past, and I've never been prescribed any medications for my feelings. Lately, it's been the complete opposite—I just have this overwhelming energy and keep jumping from one big, impulsive plan to the next.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a similar period of unusually high energy, racing thoughts, or decreased need for sleep at any other time in your life before this month?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall ever having an episode quite like this before in my life. This level of intense energy and constant planning has really only surfaced over the past month, ever since I started facing that retirement transition.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen a doctor or therapist for your mood in the past, or been prescribed any medications to help with how you were feeling?\n\nPatient: No, I've never seen a doctor or a therapist for my mood in the past, and I've never been prescribed any medications for my feelings. This kind of severe mood change is completely new to me.\n\nDoctor: During this past month, have you noticed a change in your sleep patterns, such as needing much less sleep than usual but still feeling fully rested and energized?\n\nPatient: Yes, that's exactly what's happening. I wake up after just a few hours of sleep buzzing with energy, pacing around the house and rearranging my workshop. My wife begs me to just sit down and rest, but I feel physically unable to do it.\n\nDoctor: During this high-energy period, have you experienced any unusual thoughts, such as feeling you have special powers, or hearing and seeing things that others cannot?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I don't have any special powers, and I'm not hearing or seeing things that aren't there. I just feel incredibly energetic and full of ideas, but my mind is still grounded in reality.\n\nDoctor: Have these impulsive plans or high energy levels led to severe consequences, such as major financial losses, serious conflicts at work, or situations where you felt you or others were in danger?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has caused some real problems. I've taken off on long, unplanned solo drives without telling anyone, which terrified my family and made my wife feel unsafe. I've also volunteered our limited savings to fund events I just dreamed up and missed mentoring commitments, causing a lot of tension with my wife and church leadership.\n\nDoctor: Have these behaviors completely disrupted your ability to work or manage your daily life, or have you ever required hospitalization because of them?"

Lately, it's been the complete opposite—I just have this overwhelming energy and keep jumping from one big, impulsive plan to the next.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a similar period of unusually high energy, racing thoughts, or decreased need for sleep at any other time in your life before this month?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall ever having an episode quite like this before in my life. I don't have any special powers, and I'm not hearing or seeing things that aren't there. I've also volunteered our limited savings to fund events I just dreamed up and missed mentoring commitments, causing a lot of tension with my wife and church leadership.\n\nDoctor: Have these behaviors completely disrupted your ability to work or manage your daily life, or have you ever required hospitalization because of them?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has completely disrupted my daily life; I've missed several mentoring commitments, upset church leadership with my half-baked plans, and my wife says she can't rely on me or feel safe anymore. I haven't been hospitalized for this, but the strain on my family and my life has been deeply disruptive and concerning.\n\nDoctor: During this past month, have you or others noticed that you are talking much faster than usual, or that it is difficult for people to interrupt you?"

I’m usually a very warm, steady person, but this intense phase of nonstop planning and impulsive behavior started about a month ago, right after my supervisor pushed me to set a firm retirement date.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods in your life where you felt the opposite—extremely down, exhausted, or lost interest in your usual activities for two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I don't recall ever having times in my life where I felt really down or lost interest in things like that. Lately, it's been the complete opposite—I just have this overwhelming energy and keep jumping from one big, impulsive plan to the next.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a similar period of unusually high energy, racing thoughts, or decreased need for sleep at any other time in your life before this month?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall ever having an episode quite like this before in my life. This level of intense energy and constant planning has really only surfaced over the past month, ever since I started facing that retirement transition.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen a doctor or therapist for your mood in the past, or been prescribed any medications to help with how you were feeling?\n\nPatient: No, I've never seen a doctor or a therapist for my mood in the past, and I've never been prescribed any medications for my feelings. This kind of severe mood change is completely new to me.\n\nDoctor: During this past month, have you noticed a change in your sleep patterns, such as needing much less sleep than usual but still feeling fully rested and energized?\n\nPatient: Yes, that's exactly what's happening. I wake up after just a few hours of sleep buzzing with energy, pacing around the house and rearranging my workshop. My wife begs me to just sit down and rest, but I feel physically unable to do it.\n\nDoctor: During this high-energy period, have you experienced any unusual thoughts, such as feeling you have special powers, or hearing and seeing things that others cannot?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I don't have any special powers, and I'm not hearing or seeing things that aren't there. I just feel incredibly energetic and full of ideas, but my mind is still grounded in reality.\n\nDoctor: Have these impulsive plans or high energy levels led to severe consequences, such as major financial losses, serious conflicts at work, or situations where you felt you or others were in danger?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has caused some real problems. I've taken off on long, unplanned solo drives without telling anyone, which terrified my family and made my wife feel unsafe. I've also volunteered our limited savings to fund events I just dreamed up and missed mentoring commitments, causing a lot of tension with my wife and church leadership.\n\nDoctor: Have these behaviors completely disrupted your ability to work or manage your daily life, or have you ever required hospitalization because of them?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has completely disrupted my daily life; I've missed several mentoring commitments, upset church leadership with my half-baked plans, and my wife says she can't rely on me or feel safe anymore. I haven't been hospitalized for this, but the strain on my family and my life has been deeply disruptive and concerning.\n\nDoctor: During this past month, have you or others noticed that you are talking much faster than usual, or that it is difficult for people to interrupt you?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. Once I get going, I just keep talking about everything—my retirement, new youth programs, trips, and old stories from the plant. I jump from one thing to the next so fast that people can't get a word in, and I feel this physical pressure in my chest that won't let me stop.\n\nDoctor: Are you currently taking any medications for physical health conditions, or have you recently started using any new substances, alcohol, or over-the-counter drugs?"
  }

No, I'm not currently taking any major medications for my physical health. I also haven't started using any new substances, over-the-counter drugs, or alcohol recently.

Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.
